I know that probably sounds odd. I used to just go for the coffee after service, you know, and talk about who made the casserole or whose granddaughter got married, but lately I sit there reading things twice and wondering what they’re really trying to say. It’s been hard to shake.

Not so much TV or songs, no… I mean, I don’t watch as much as I used to, except sometimes the evening news if I remember to turn it on. My neighbor used to have the radio going all the time, old hymns and those call-in shows, and I’d hear bits through the wall, but that’s not really what’s been bothering me.

It’s more at church, and with the walking club. Even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some people there are watching me, or that little messages about me are being put in the bulletin or slipped into what they say. Like a phrase will stand out, and I’ll think, “Why did they say it like that?” and then I’m stuck on it.

I don’t know about the weather so much… I mean, if it rains on walking-club day I mostly just think, well, now my shoes will get damp, and I’ve got that one pair that never dries right.

I try to tell myself it’s probably nothing, but then I keep thinking about it.

Like a phrase will stand out, and I’ll think, “Why did they say it like that?” and then I’m stuck on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like the way people dress, the weather, or things happening around you, have a special meaning or connection to you personally?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about the weather so much… I mean, if it rains on walking-club day I mostly just think, well, now my shoes will get damp, and I’ve got that one pair that never dries right.\n\nBut the things happening around me, yes… mostly at church and with the walking club. That’s really why I came in, because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t shake this feeling that some people are watching me. People I don’t even know working together… I don’t think of it that big, exactly. It’s not like the whole town or anything.

It’s more why I came in, really. Even when I’m doing a little better, I can’t shake this feeling that some people at church, and sometimes in the walking club, are watching me. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or moving my body around, nothing like that. I still feel like myself.

Yes… I mean, that’s sort of why I came in. I know other people don’t seem to notice it, or they’d probably tell me I’m making too much of it.

a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a spell of several weeks when I was getting dressed, eating a bit better, and not feeling so weighed down, but I still heard my name in the house and thought people at church were hinting about me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I still live at home, but I have stopped keeping up with quilting gatherings, walking club, and much of my gardening, and my daughter has started checking on my meals and appointments more often.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The deep sadness, guilt, and loss of interest have been with me for about a year ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I did tell someone at church once, sort of. Not the whole thing, because it sounds… you know. I just said it felt like people were watching me, and she said, “Oh, I’m sure nobody means anything by it.” She was trying to be kind.

But even after that, I still couldn’t really let it go. I came in because even on my better days, I keep feeling like some people at church, and sometimes in the walking club too, are watching me or putting little messages about me in the bulletin, or in the way they say things. And then I’ll be home doing something ordinary, like rinsing a mug or looking for my reading glasses, and it’s still sitting there in my mind.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22.0, F20.0, F23.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I came in because I keep getting this feeling, even when I’m having a decent day, that people at church, and sometimes in my walking club, are watching me. Like they’ll say something a certain way, or there’ll be something in the church bulletin, and it feels like it’s a little message about me.\n\nI know that probably sounds odd. I used to just go for the coffee after service, you know, and talk about who made the casserole or whose granddaughter got married, but lately I sit there reading things twice and wondering what they’re really trying to say. It’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are sending you hidden messages, do you ever feel that certain things in the environment, like words in the bulletin or phrases people say, are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s what I mean. Like the bulletin will have a phrase in it, and I know it’s just supposed to be church news, but I’ll read it and feel like, “That’s about me.” Or someone in the walking club says something while we’re out, and it feels like they’re hinting at something, like they’re watching how I react.\n\nIt isn’t every single thing, but even on better days I can’t quite shake it. I’ll tell myself I’m probably reading too much into it, and then I’ll go back and look again. It just feels directed at me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you felt that things people say, or things you see in the media or environment, are directly related to you, even if others don’t seem to notice it?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s been happening. Mostly at church and with the walking club. I’ll see something in the bulletin or hear someone say a little phrase, and it feels like it’s meant for me somehow… like they’re watching me or putting in a message about me.\n\nAnd I know other people probably just read it and move on. My friend Marlene, she always just checks the bake sale notice and the prayer list—she doesn’t seem to see anything unusual. But I’ll sit there thinking, “Why did they word it that way?” Even on days when I’m doing alright, I can’t quite shake that feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say on TV, in songs, or in public places like stores are also directed at you or have a special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Not so much TV or songs, no… I mean, I don’t watch as much as I used to, except sometimes the evening news if I remember to turn it on. My neighbor used to have the radio going all the time, old hymns and those call-in shows, and I’d hear bits through the wall, but that’s not really what’s been bothering me.\n\nIt’s more at church, and with the walking club. Even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some people there are watching me, or that little messages about me are being put in the bulletin or slipped into what they say. Like a phrase will stand out, and I’ll think, “Why did they say it like that?” and then I’m stuck on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like the way people dress, the weather, or things happening around you, have a special meaning or connection to you personally?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about the weather so much… I mean, if it rains on walking-club day I mostly just think, well, now my shoes will get damp, and I’ve got that one pair that never dries right.\n\nBut the things happening around me, yes… mostly at church and with the walking club. That’s really why I came in, because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t shake this feeling that some people are watching me. Like they’re putting little messages about me in the bulletin, or slipping things into what they say, and I’m supposed to notice. I try to tell myself it’s probably nothing, but then I keep thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that strangers or people you don’t know well, like those in the grocery store or passing by on the street, are also sending you hidden messages or watching you in some way?\n\nPatient: Not really strangers so much… like, if I’m at the grocery store, I mostly just want to get my milk and get out before I forget something. Though sometimes I do notice people looking, but I don’t know them, so it doesn’t stick the same way.\n\nIt’s more the people at church and the walking club. That’s what’s been bothering me. Even when I’m having a better day, I still get this feeling that some of them are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in something they say. I’ll try to brush it off, but then I keep turning it over in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that are very strange or unusual, like people you don’t even know are working together to watch you or send you messages in some way?\n\nPatient: People I don’t even know working together… I don’t think of it that big, exactly. It’s not like the whole town or anything.\n\nIt’s more why I came in, really. Even when I’m doing a little better, I can’t shake this feeling that some people at church, and sometimes in the walking club, are watching me. Like there are little messages about me tucked into the bulletin, or in the way somebody says something. And then I’ll go home and keep thinking about it while I’m making tea or trying to fold the towels, and I know that sounds strange, but it feels hard to let go of.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people you don't personally know, like those on TV, public figures, or groups in society, are involved in sending you messages or watching you in some way?\n\nPatient: No, not really TV people or public figures… I don’t follow all that as much, except I do leave the TV on sometimes while I’m doing the dishes, and then I realize I haven’t heard a word of it.\n\nIt’s more the people I actually see, like at church and the walking club. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Mostly it’s those places… church, and the walking club. Or certain people from there, I suppose. I don’t usually feel it, say, at the grocery store or when I’m just walking to the mailbox.

But then again, I’ll come home and it follows me in my head, if that makes sense. I’ll be making tea or sorting the mail, and I’ll start thinking about the bulletin again, or something someone said on the walk, like maybe it was meant for me. So it starts there, but it doesn’t always stay there in my mind.

Like the bulletin will have a phrase in it, and I know it’s just supposed to be church news, but I’ll read it and feel like, “That’s about me.” Or someone in the walking club says something while we’re out, and it feels like they’re hinting at something, like they’re watching how I react.\n\nIt isn’t every single thing, but even on better days I can’t quite shake it. I’ll tell myself I’m probably reading too much into it, and then I’ll go back and look again. My friend Marlene, she always just checks the bake sale notice and the prayer list—she doesn’t seem to see anything unusual. That’s really why I came in, because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t shake this feeling that some people are watching me. Like they’re putting little messages about me in the bulletin, or slipping things into what they say, and I’m supposed to notice. But I’ll sit there thinking, “Why did they word it that way?” Even on days when I’m doing alright, I can’t quite shake that feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say on TV, in songs, or in public places like stores are also directed at you or have a special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Not so much TV or songs, no… I mean, I don’t watch as much as I used to, except sometimes the evening news if I remember to turn it on. My neighbor used to have the radio going all the time, old hymns and those call-in shows, and I’d hear bits through the wall, but that’s not really what’s been bothering me.\n\nIt’s more at church, and with the walking club. Even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some people there are watching me, or that little messages about me are being put in the bulletin or slipped into what they say. Like a phrase will stand out, and I’ll think, “Why did they say it like that?” and then I’m stuck on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like the way people dress, the weather, or things happening around you, have a special meaning or connection to you personally?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about the weather so much… I mean, if it rains on walking-club day I mostly just think, well, now my shoes will get damp, and I’ve got that one pair that never dries right.\n\nBut the things happening around me, yes… mostly at church and with the walking club. That’s really why I came in, because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t shake this feeling that some people are watching me. Like they’re putting little messages about me in the bulletin, or slipping things into what they say, and I’m supposed to notice. I try to tell myself it’s probably nothing, but then I keep thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that strangers or people you don’t know well, like those in the grocery store or passing by on the street, are also sending you hidden messages or watching you in some way?\n\nPatient: Not really strangers so much… like, if I’m at the grocery store, I mostly just want to get my milk and get out before I forget something. Though sometimes I do notice people looking, but I don’t know them, so it doesn’t stick the same way.\n\nIt’s more the people at church and the walking club. That’s what’s been bothering me. Even when I’m having a better day, I still get this feeling that some of them are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in something they say. I’ll try to brush it off, but then I keep turning it over in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that are very strange or unusual, like people you don’t even know are working together to watch you or send you messages in some way?\n\nPatient: People I don’t even know working together… I don’t think of it that big, exactly. It’s not like the whole town or anything.\n\nIt’s more why I came in, really. Even when I’m doing a little better, I can’t shake this feeling that some people at church, and sometimes in the walking club, are watching me. Like there are little messages about me tucked into the bulletin, or in the way somebody says something. And then I’ll go home and keep thinking about it while I’m making tea or trying to fold the towels, and I know that sounds strange, but it feels hard to let go of.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people you don't personally know, like those on TV, public figures, or groups in society, are involved in sending you messages or watching you in some way?\n\nPatient: No, not really TV people or public figures… I don’t follow all that as much, except I do leave the TV on sometimes while I’m doing the dishes, and then I realize I haven’t heard a word of it.\n\nIt’s more the people I actually see, like at church and the walking club. That’s what made me come in. Even on better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some of them are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or in how they say things. Then I go home and keep thinking about it, and it’s hard to settle myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that something very strange or unusual is happening to you, like someone is putting thoughts in your mind, changing your body, or controlling your actions without your control?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or moving my body around, nothing like that. I still feel like myself.\n\nIt’s more… well, like I said, the church bulletin, and sometimes people in the walking club. Even when I’m having a halfway decent day, I can’t shake the feeling they’re watching me or slipping little messages about me into what they say. I’ll try to tell myself I’m being silly, then I’m home making tea and it’s still going around in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any thoughts that seem very strange or unusual to you, like believing something is happening that others don't seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, that’s sort of why I came in. I know other people don’t seem to notice it, or they’d probably tell me I’m making too much of it.\n\nIt’s mostly at church, and sometimes with the walking club. I get this feeling like certain people are watching me, or like there are little messages about me in the bulletin, or in the way someone says something. And I’ll try to brush it off, you know, I’ll go home and make tea or start folding laundry, and then I’m still thinking about it.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as strange, but it feels strange to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you’ve shared these feelings with someone, like a friend or family member, and they’ve tried to reassure you that it’s not true, but you still couldn’t shake the feeling that it was real?\n\nPatient: Yes… I did tell someone at church once, sort of. Not the whole thing, because it sounds… you know. I just said it felt like people were watching me, and she said, “Oh, I’m sure nobody means anything by it.” She was trying to be kind.\n\nBut even after that, I still couldn’t really let it go. I came in because even on my better days, I keep feeling like some people at church, and sometimes in the walking club too, are watching me or putting little messages about me in the bulletin, or in the way they say things. And then I’ll be home doing something ordinary, like rinsing a mug or looking for my reading glasses, and it’s still sitting there in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Are the messages or feelings of being watched limited to specific places or people, like church and the walking club, or do they sometimes spread to other parts of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s those places… church, and the walking club. Or certain people from there, I suppose. I don’t usually feel it, say, at the grocery store or when I’m just walking to the mailbox.\n\nBut then again, I’ll come home and it follows me in my head, if that makes sense. I’ll be making tea or sorting the mail, and I’ll start thinking about the bulletin again, or something someone said on the walk, like maybe it was meant for me. So it starts there, but it doesn’t always stay there in my mind.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. That’s what made me come in. Even on better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some of them are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or in how they say things. Then I go home and keep thinking about it, and it’s hard to settle myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that something very strange or unusual is happening to you, like someone is putting thoughts in your mind, changing your body, or controlling your actions without your control?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or moving my body around, nothing like that. I still feel like myself.\n\nIt’s more… well, like I said, the church bulletin, and sometimes people in the walking club. Even when I’m having a halfway decent day, I can’t shake the feeling they’re watching me or slipping little messages about me into what they say. I’ll try to tell myself I’m being silly, then I’m home making tea and it’s still going around in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any thoughts that seem very strange or unusual to you, like believing something is happening that others don't seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, that’s sort of why I came in. I know other people don’t seem to notice it, or they’d probably tell me I’m making too much of it.\n\nIt’s mostly at church, and sometimes with the walking club. I get this feeling like certain people are watching me, or like there are little messages about me in the bulletin, or in the way someone says something. And I’ll try to brush it off, you know, I’ll go home and make tea or start folding laundry, and then I’m still thinking about it.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as strange, but it feels strange to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you’ve shared these feelings with someone, like a friend or family member, and they’ve tried to reassure you that it’s not true, but you still couldn’t shake the feeling that it was real?\n\nPatient: Yes… I did tell someone at church once, sort of. Not the whole thing, because it sounds… you know. I just said it felt like people were watching me, and she said, “Oh, I’m sure nobody means anything by it.” She was trying to be kind.\n\nBut even after that, I still couldn’t really let it go. I came in because even on my better days, I keep feeling like some people at church, and sometimes in the walking club too, are watching me or putting little messages about me in the bulletin, or in the way they say things. And then I’ll be home doing something ordinary, like rinsing a mug or looking for my reading glasses, and it’s still sitting there in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Are the messages or feelings of being watched limited to specific places or people, like church and the walking club, or do they sometimes spread to other parts of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s those places… church, and the walking club. Or certain people from there, I suppose. I don’t usually feel it, say, at the grocery store or when I’m just walking to the mailbox.\n\nBut then again, I’ll come home and it follows me in my head, if that makes sense. I’ll be making tea or sorting the mail, and I’ll start thinking about the bulletin again, or something someone said on the walk, like maybe it was meant for me. So it starts there, but it doesn’t always stay there in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel people are sending you messages or watching you, even when there’s no specific thing that happened or was said to make you feel that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes there isn’t one clear thing I can point to. It’s more like I’m already on edge about it, and then I’ll just have that feeling that they’re watching me, or that there’s something meant for me in the bulletin or in what someone says.\n\nI mean, I came in because even on my better days I can’t really shake it. Like with church, or the walking club… and then I’ll be trying to do something normal at home, like put away dishes, and I’m still thinking, “Was that about me?” It sounds silly when I say it, but it feels real at the time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Like with church, or the walking club… and then I’ll be trying to do something normal at home, like put away dishes, and I’m still thinking, “Was that about me?” It sounds silly when I say it, but it feels real at the time.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had any experiences that you know are unusual, like believing that a large group of people are working together in secret to send you messages or that something is happening that no one else seems to notice?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 66 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because even on my better days I can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say a large group, exactly… that sounds too big when you put it that way. But I do feel like some people at church, and sometimes in the walking club, are sort of… watching me, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in the way they say things.

Even when I’m having a decent day, I can’t seem to shake it. I’ll try to tell myself maybe it’s nothing, and then I’ll remember a phrase from the bulletin, or something someone said while we were walking, and it feels like it was meant for me. It’s hard because I know it sounds unusual, but in the moment it feels very real.

Like they’ll say something a certain way, or there’ll be something in the church bulletin, and it feels like it’s a little message about me.\n\nI know that probably sounds odd. I used to just go for the coffee after service, you know, and talk about who made the casserole or whose granddaughter got married, but lately I sit there reading things twice and wondering what they’re really trying to say. It’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are sending you hidden messages, do you ever feel that certain things in the environment, like words in the bulletin or phrases people say, are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s what I mean. Like the bulletin will have a phrase in it, and I know it’s just supposed to be church news, but I’ll read it and feel like, “That’s about me.” Or someone in the walking club says something while we’re out, and it feels like they’re hinting at something, like they’re watching how I react.\n\nIt isn’t every single thing, but even on better days I can’t quite shake it. I’ll tell myself I’m probably reading too much into it, and then I’ll go back and look again. It just feels directed at me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you felt that things people say, or things you see in the media or environment, are directly related to you, even if others don’t seem to notice it?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s been happening. My neighbor used to have the radio going all the time, old hymns and those call-in shows, and I’d hear bits through the wall, but that’s not really what’s been bothering me.\n\nIt’s more at church, and with the walking club. Even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some people there are watching me, or that little messages about me are being put in the bulletin or slipped into what they say. Like a phrase will stand out, and I’ll think, “Why did they say it like that?” and then I’m stuck on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like the way people dress, the weather, or things happening around you, have a special meaning or connection to you personally?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about the weather so much… I mean, if it rains on walking-club day I mostly just think, well, now my shoes will get damp, and I’ve got that one pair that never dries right.\n\nBut the things happening around me, yes… mostly at church and with the walking club. That’s really why I came in, because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t shake this feeling that some people are watching me. Like they’re putting little messages about me in the bulletin, or slipping things into what they say, and I’m supposed to notice. I try to tell myself it’s probably nothing, but then I keep thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that strangers or people you don’t know well, like those in the grocery store or passing by on the street, are also sending you hidden messages or watching you in some way?\n\nPatient: Not really strangers so much… like, if I’m at the grocery store, I mostly just want to get my milk and get out before I forget something. I’ll try to brush it off, but then I keep turning it over in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that are very strange or unusual, like people you don’t even know are working together to watch you or send you messages in some way?\n\nPatient: People I don’t even know working together… I don’t think of it that big, exactly. Even when I’m doing a little better, I can’t shake this feeling that some people at church, and sometimes in the walking club, are watching me. Like there are little messages about me tucked into the bulletin, or in the way somebody says something. And then I’ll go home and keep thinking about it while I’m making tea or trying to fold the towels, and I know that sounds strange, but it feels hard to let go of.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people you don't personally know, like those on TV, public figures, or groups in society, are involved in sending you messages or watching you in some way?\n\nPatient: No, not really TV people or public figures… I don’t follow all that as much, except I do leave the TV on sometimes while I’m doing the dishes, and then I realize I haven’t heard a word of it.\n\nIt’s more the people I actually see, like at church and the walking club. That’s what made me come in. Even on better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some of them are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or in how they say things. Then I go home and keep thinking about it, and it’s hard to settle myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that something very strange or unusual is happening to you, like someone is putting thoughts in your mind, changing your body, or controlling your actions without your control?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or moving my body around, nothing like that. I still feel like myself.\n\nIt’s more… well, like I said, the church bulletin, and sometimes people in the walking club. I wouldn’t say completely certain every single minute… like, if my sister says, “Oh, you’re reading too much into it,” I can nod along for a while. And then I’ll go home and be folding laundry or looking at the church bulletin again, and there it is, that feeling.

I used to just go for the coffee after service, you know, and talk about who made the casserole or whose granddaughter got married, but lately I sit there reading things twice and wondering what they’re really trying to say. It’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are sending you hidden messages, do you ever feel that certain things in the environment, like words in the bulletin or phrases people say, are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s what I mean. Like the bulletin will have a phrase in it, and I know it’s just supposed to be church news, but I’ll read it and feel like, “That’s about me.” Or someone in the walking club says something while we’re out, and it feels like they’re hinting at something, like they’re watching how I react.\n\nIt isn’t every single thing, but even on better days I can’t quite shake it. I’ll tell myself I’m probably reading too much into it, and then I’ll go back and look again. It just feels directed at me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you felt that things people say, or things you see in the media or environment, are directly related to you, even if others don’t seem to notice it?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s been happening. Mostly at church and with the walking club. I’ll see something in the bulletin or hear someone say a little phrase, and it feels like it’s meant for me somehow… like they’re watching me or putting in a message about me.\n\nAnd I know other people probably just read it and move on. My friend Marlene, she always just checks the bake sale notice and the prayer list—she doesn’t seem to see anything unusual. But I’ll sit there thinking, “Why did they word it that way?” Even on days when I’m doing alright, I can’t quite shake that feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say on TV, in songs, or in public places like stores are also directed at you or have a special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Not so much TV or songs, no… I mean, I don’t watch as much as I used to, except sometimes the evening news if I remember to turn it on. My neighbor used to have the radio going all the time, old hymns and those call-in shows, and I’d hear bits through the wall, but that’s not really what’s been bothering me.\n\nIt’s more at church, and with the walking club. Even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some people there are watching me, or that little messages about me are being put in the bulletin or slipped into what they say. Like a phrase will stand out, and I’ll think, “Why did they say it like that?” and then I’m stuck on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like the way people dress, the weather, or things happening around you, have a special meaning or connection to you personally?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about the weather so much… I mean, if it rains on walking-club day I mostly just think, well, now my shoes will get damp, and I’ve got that one pair that never dries right.\n\nBut the things happening around me, yes… mostly at church and with the walking club. That’s really why I came in, because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t shake this feeling that some people are watching me. Like they’re putting little messages about me in the bulletin, or slipping things into what they say, and I’m supposed to notice. I try to tell myself it’s probably nothing, but then I keep thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that strangers or people you don’t know well, like those in the grocery store or passing by on the street, are also sending you hidden messages or watching you in some way?\n\nPatient: Not really strangers so much… like, if I’m at the grocery store, I mostly just want to get my milk and get out before I forget something. Though sometimes I do notice people looking, but I don’t know them, so it doesn’t stick the same way.\n\nIt’s more the people at church and the walking club. That’s what’s been bothering me. Even when I’m having a better day, I still get this feeling that some of them are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in something they say. I’ll try to brush it off, but then I keep turning it over in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that are very strange or unusual, like people you don’t even know are working together to watch you or send you messages in some way?\n\nPatient: People I don’t even know working together… I don’t think of it that big, exactly. It’s not like the whole town or anything.\n\nIt’s more why I came in, really. Even when I’m doing a little better, I can’t shake this feeling that some people at church, and sometimes in the walking club, are watching me. Like there are little messages about me tucked into the bulletin, or in the way somebody says something. And then I’ll go home and keep thinking about it while I’m making tea or trying to fold the towels, and I know that sounds strange, but it feels hard to let go of.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people you don't personally know, like those on TV, public figures, or groups in society, are involved in sending you messages or watching you in some way?\n\nPatient: No, not really TV people or public figures… I don’t follow all that as much, except I do leave the TV on sometimes while I’m doing the dishes, and then I realize I haven’t heard a word of it.\n\nIt’s more the people I actually see, like at church and the walking club. That’s what made me come in. Even on better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some of them are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or in how they say things. Then I go home and keep thinking about it, and it’s hard to settle myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that something very strange or unusual is happening to you, like someone is putting thoughts in your mind, changing your body, or controlling your actions without your control?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or moving my body around, nothing like that. I still feel like myself.\n\nIt’s more… well, like I said, the church bulletin, and sometimes people in the walking club. Even when I’m having a halfway decent day, I can’t shake the feeling they’re watching me or slipping little messages about me into what they say. I’ll try to tell myself I’m being silly, then I’m home making tea and it’s still going around in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any thoughts that seem very strange or unusual to you, like believing something is happening that others don't seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, that’s sort of why I came in. I know other people don’t seem to notice it, or they’d probably tell me I’m making too much of it.\n\nIt’s mostly at church, and sometimes with the walking club. I get this feeling like certain people are watching me, or like there are little messages about me in the bulletin, or in the way someone says something. And I’ll try to brush it off, you know, I’ll go home and make tea or start folding laundry, and then I’m still thinking about it.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as strange, but it feels strange to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you’ve shared these feelings with someone, like a friend or family member, and they’ve tried to reassure you that it’s not true, but you still couldn’t shake the feeling that it was real?\n\nPatient: Yes… I did tell someone at church once, sort of. Not the whole thing, because it sounds… you know. I just said it felt like people were watching me, and she said, “Oh, I’m sure nobody means anything by it.” She was trying to be kind.\n\nBut even after that, I still couldn’t really let it go. I came in because even on my better days, I keep feeling like some people at church, and sometimes in the walking club too, are watching me or putting little messages about me in the bulletin, or in the way they say things. And then I’ll be home doing something ordinary, like rinsing a mug or looking for my reading glasses, and it’s still sitting there in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Are the messages or feelings of being watched limited to specific places or people, like church and the walking club, or do they sometimes spread to other parts of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s those places… church, and the walking club. Or certain people from there, I suppose. I don’t usually feel it, say, at the grocery store or when I’m just walking to the mailbox.\n\nBut then again, I’ll come home and it follows me in my head, if that makes sense. I’ll be making tea or sorting the mail, and I’ll start thinking about the bulletin again, or something someone said on the walk, like maybe it was meant for me. So it starts there, but it doesn’t always stay there in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel people are sending you messages or watching you, even when there’s no specific thing that happened or was said to make you feel that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes there isn’t one clear thing I can point to. It’s more like I’m already on edge about it, and then I’ll just have that feeling that they’re watching me, or that there’s something meant for me in the bulletin or in what someone says.\n\nI mean, I came in because even on my better days I can’t really shake it. Like with church, or the walking club… and then I’ll be trying to do something normal at home, like put away dishes, and I’m still thinking, “Was that about me?” It sounds silly when I say it, but it feels real at the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences that you know are unusual, like believing that a large group of people are working together in secret to send you messages or that something is happening that no one else seems to notice?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say a large group, exactly… that sounds too big when you put it that way. But I do feel like some people at church, and sometimes in the walking club, are sort of… watching me, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in the way they say things.\n\nEven when I’m having a decent day, I can’t seem to shake it. I’ll try to tell myself maybe it’s nothing, and then I’ll remember a phrase from the bulletin, or something someone said while we were walking, and it feels like it was meant for me. It’s hard because I know it sounds unusual, but in the moment it feels very real.\n\nDoctor: Even when others have tried to reassure you or when there’s no clear reason to think someone is sending you messages, do you still feel completely certain that these messages or signs are real and meant for you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say completely certain every single minute… like, if my sister says, “Oh, you’re reading too much into it,” I can nod along for a while. And then I’ll go home and be folding laundry or looking at the church bulletin again, and there it is, that feeling.\n\nThat’s actually why I came in, because even on better days I can’t quite shake it — that some people at church, and in the walking club too, are watching me somehow, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. It’s not like I want to think that. I try to distract myself, make tea, do the crossword… though I’m terrible at the Sunday one. But it keeps coming back feeling like it’s meant for me."
